Money edits are flagged `material`, mirrored from Rob's ledger, never originated. 21 tests, 723/723 vitest, build green. Four lie-shaped failures closed: apply happens BEFORE record (a run row can never claim writes that did not land) and a throwing write is still recorded as a refusal naming the error; a READ failure records nothing at all (`content_sha256` is NOT NULL — with no bytes there is no digest, and a faked one is worse than a missing run, so the previous run stays the newest); an unreadable STORE is never treated as an empty one (diffing against a failed load would withdraw every invoice prod holds); writes that land under a lost run row return `unrecorded`, loudly, not success. Malformed digest aborts before any write. A zero-change run is still recorded — proving the sync ran today is most of the audit table's point. `applyPlan` exposes no delete at all. 750/750 vitest, build green. NO DEPLOY — panel still `unavailable`, test-pinned. **Q59's tick now needs exactly ONE thing: the ADAPTERS + SCHEDULE** — fs read of `../contracts/invoices/invoice-ledger.csv`, sha256, `git rev-parse` (null when dirty), service-role writes to the two 0012 tables, cron; that increment carries the MC.11 workflow-map row (nothing exists to map until a workflow exists). **ATLAS: Architecture Atlas refresh pass still owed (dated 2026-07-24/25) — `lib/readModel/` is now SIX modules and prod holds two new tables; republish requires the interactive session.** **7/25 inc.9 (driver): MC.9 HALF 2 ADAPTERS SHIPPED (PRD 3.1.113)** — `lib/readModel/ledgerAdapters.ts`, the concrete ports the runner shell was built to receive: fs+git source (bytes read, THOSE bytes digested; `git rev-parse` reported only when the file is clean at HEAD — dirty tree, unresolvable rev or missing git ⇒ `null`, never a nearby revision that did not produce these bytes) and service-role store (loads withdrawn rows too so a reappearing invoice diffs against what we hold; **throws instead of returning `[]`** on a failed load, since an empty array there would withdraw every invoice in prod; marks withdrawals one at a time so a partial failure names the invoice it stopped on; no delete exists on the port). No policy moved — the conservative rules stay in the decision core. The real risk here is the silent one: camelCase→snake_case is the one seam where a dropped key **does not error, it writes NULL**, and an invoice loses its due date on a money panel with every test still green. Permissions as Supabase RLS (2–3 days, value 5) — gates Phase 4.** Take Macro's `entity_access` table shape (9 columns, polymorphic subject, `granted_from_*` recording WHY a grant exists so a cascade revokes exactly the inherited ones, two partial unique indexes closing a NULL-duplication hole) and INVERT their enforcement point: they check in application code copy-pasted across 10+ query files behind a 30s stale cache; put it in RLS where it cannot be forgotten. Shipped: `supabase/migrations/0017_entity_access.sql` (9-column grant table, two partial unique indexes, three lookup indexes, a provenance-pair CHECK, `access_level_rank()`, and the revocation triggers) + `lib/entityAccess.ts` (pure per CR-3) + 25 tests. **SCOPE — do not over-read it:** this is the DATA half only. **Nothing enforces access yet** — no policy, no route, and **zero grants exist** on prod. An inert grant table cannot lock Rob out of his own dashboard. **THE TICK NEEDS EXACTLY ONE THING: the RLS policies + the non-owner-read-returns-zero-rows test** — the next increment. **Three Macro decisions verbatim:** polymorphic *subject* (user/team/role) so one table says "Rob can edit"/"sales can view"/"every rep can comment"; `granted_from_*` provenance so a cascade revokes exactly the inherited grants; two partial unique indexes because Postgres treats NULL as distinct (one constraint would allow unlimited duplicate *direct* grants). **Four divergences on purpose:** (a) **enforcement inverted into RLS** — Macro checks in app code copy-pasted across 10+ query files behind a 30s stale cache; a check in call sites is a check somebody forgets, and this inversion is the whole reason Q66 exists; (b) provenance is polymorphic so Postgres **cannot** cascade it — the cascade is written as our own delete triggers on people/orgs/deals (one function, kind via `TG_ARGV[0]`, 0016's pattern); without them the FK-less design leaks silently; (c) CHECK text not ENUMs (0015 precedent); (d) no `dashboard_ro` grant. **The bug this pins:** comparing levels as raw strings sorts alphabetically — `comment < edit < owner < view` — putting **view above owner**, so a `>=` on text grants a viewer everything and denies an owner nothing, silently. Ladder is a function on both sides, parse-compared rank-for-rank, and `'view' >= 'owner' === true` is pinned in a test. Unknown level → rank 0, fails closed. **RLS shipped with the table** (anon key is in the client bundle; an anon-writable ACL means anyone grants themselves owner). **Prod-verified, not claimed:** tracked Supabase MCP apply; `rls_on=true`, 3 triggers, 6 indexes; aborted-transaction probe proved `direct_ok → dup_rejected 23505 → half_pair_rejected 23514 → bad_level_rejected 23514 → inherited coexists with direct`; a second aborted probe created a **synthetic** person, hung 3 grants off it, deleted it, and measured the cascade — **own 0, inherited 0, unrelated direct 1** (exact, not greedy). Both rolled back: 0 grants, 0 probe rows, 22 people. **No real record deleted** — the only delete was a row the probe itself created inside an aborted transaction. 931/931 vitest, build green; the "inert" claim is test-pinned (`no create policy in 0017`). **NO DEPLOY** — zero user-visible surface. No money/signed/quoted/paid field touched; `STORAGE_SOURCE` untouched. **ATLAS: Architecture Atlas needs its refresh pass (dated 2026-07-25) — it must now draw `entity_access` as live prod schema, labelled INERT until the policy increment lands.** **7/25 inc.17 (driver): ✅ TICKED — the DoD is met verbatim: policies + a test that a non-owner read returns zero rows.** dev_chat re-queried first — newest rob **#47** < newest max **#49**, no unanswered Rob instruction, nothing posted; Q64 above is still BLOCKED-ON-ROB ("do not decide this for him"), so Q66 was top actionable. Shipped `supabase/migrations/0018_entity_access_policies.sql` (PRD 3.1.155): `current_access_subjects()` (JWT → user/team/role subjects, the SQL mirror of `expandSubjects()`), `has_entity_access()` as the **SECURITY DEFINER** predicate with `search_path` pinned to public, and **SELECT policies on people / orgs / deals + a self-service policy on `entity_access`** — the enforcement inversion 0017 promised, now real: the check lives in RLS where a forgotten call site fails CLOSED, not in app code copy-pasted across 10+ query files like Macro. **WHY THIS COULD NOT LOCK ROB OUT (the reason it was safe to apply tonight):** people (0001) / deals (0005) / orgs (0006) *already* had RLS on with **zero** policies, and RLS-on + no-policy = deny-everything, so the anon path already returned zero rows; a permissive SELECT policy can only WIDEN that, and with zero grants on prod the observable behaviour is byte-identical — the difference is a grant now MEANS something. Every dashboard route uses service_role, which bypasses RLS entirely; **prod's open-read posture is untouched, because closing it is Q64 and Q64 is Rob's call.** **DELIBERATELY EXCLUDED, stated so nobody over-reads the tick:** no write policies (insert/update/delete stay service-role-only — an authorship model before real identities exist would be inventing a decision Rob hasn't made), no route wiring, no user records, and **still zero grants on prod**. **Prod-verified, not claimed** (tracked Supabase MCP apply; 4 policies, 0 non-SELECT, predicate confirmed DEFINER): a 7-step probe in an **aborted** transaction returned `stranger 0 / grantee 1 / no-JWT 0 / anon-whole-table 0 / view-grant-vs-owner 0 / typo-level 0 / service-role 23 (22 real + the probe's own row)`. Arm 2 is what makes arm 1 mean something — a policy that denies everybody would also return zero. Rolled back: **22 people, 0 probe rows, 0 grants, 0 orphan property rows**; no real record written or deleted. The probe is **checked in** at `supabase/tests/0018_entity_access_rls_proof.sql` so it is repeatable by paste rather than remembered from a transcript. **10 new tests / 941 vitest / build green** — the static half pins what SQL can't be run for in CI: every level literal in every policy is a real level, every entity kind is a real kind, `current_access_subjects()` emits exactly `SUBJECT_TYPES` (no extras — a subject kind SQL can mint but the app can't audit), the `rank(required) > 0` guard exists (**a typo'd level like `'read'` ranks 0, and without that guard `rank(held) >= 0` is true for every row — a total breach from one string literal, with no database error anywhere**), ranking is never string comparison, DEFINER + pinned search_path present, and **SELECT-only** is asserted so a later increment can't quietly add a write policy under this tick. **NO DEPLOY** — zero user-visible surface; Q52/Q53/MC.8-inc.1 precedent. No money/signed/quoted/paid field touched, nothing deleted, `STORAGE_SOURCE` untouched. **ATLAS: Architecture Atlas needs its refresh pass (dated 2026-07-25) — `entity_access` is no longer INERT; it must now be drawn as the live enforcement edge feeding SELECT policies on people/orgs/deals, with the service-role bypass shown explicitly so nobody reads the dashboard as gated.**

Next increment: the `saved_views` migration + `Expr` validator/parser at the trust boundary. 📐 ATLAS 2026-07-25: refresh pass must draw `lib/filters/ast.ts` as the property spine's first consumer (containment read) and must NOT yet draw any UI/route edge into it. **7/25 inc.2 (driver): THE TRUST BOUNDARY EXISTS — `lib/filters/parse.ts` (PRD 3.1.157), and inc.1 had a real defect that writing it exposed.** dev_chat re-queried first — newest rob **#47** < newest max **#49**, nothing posted; Q64 above still BLOCKED-ON-ROB, so Q67 was top actionable. **Why the parser preceded the migration:** `compile()` takes an `Expr`, and TypeScript guarantees that shape only for trees written in our own source — a saved view arrives as parsed JSONB and a share link as base64url'd JSON typed by a stranger, so the validator must exist *before* the first untrusted producer, not after it. **The defect, found and fixed:** `person.evil` passed the cross-target check on its prefix, missed `COLUMNS`, and compiled to the SQL text **`people.undefined = $1`** — value still bound (not injectable), but a filter error deferred to a Postgres column error, i.e. a 500 on a rep's own saved view instead of a clean rejection. `LITERAL_NAMES` is now derived from `COLUMNS` so they cannot drift, and an unknown literal throws. **Three parser rules:** rebuild never cast (fresh object literals, grammar keys only — a `__proto__` payload yields exactly `['lit','value']` and pollutes nothing); bound the tree not just its depth (`MAX_NODES=512` + a 64KB pre-`JSON.parse` byte cap — depth 32 does NOT bound a *flat* `and` with 200k args, which is depth 1); structure here, values there (no duplicated value checks — `compile()` re-validates at bind time and a second copy is a second place to drift). Empty `and`/`or` parse fine so an empty view round-trips; errors carry a JSON path (`$.args[1]: …`); `isFilterInputError()` spans both layers for one 400. **22 new tests, 975 vitest, build green, NO DEPLOY** (pure lib, nothing calls it). No money/signed/quoted/paid field touched, nothing deleted, no migration applied, `STORAGE_SOURCE` untouched. **STAYS UNTICKED** — DoD still needs the `saved_views` migration (B6) + a server-side paginated route; that migration is the next increment. 📐 ATLAS 2026-07-25: refresh pass must also draw `lib/filters/parse.ts` as the boundary node every future route/share-link edge passes through before reaching `ast.ts` — and still NO UI/route edge into either. **7/25 inc.3 (driver): THE VIEW IS A ROW, AND THE SHARE LINK IS THE SAME OBJECT — `supabase/migrations/0019_saved_views.sql` APPLIED TO PROD + `lib/filters/savedViews.ts` (PRD 3.1.158).** dev_chat re-queried first — newest rob **#47** < newest max **#49**, nothing posted; Q64 above still BLOCKED-ON-ROB, so Q67 was top actionable. **B6 taken verbatim: views are ROWS, not a `views JSONB[]` column** — a whole-list column means renaming one view rewrites the list, so two reps saving in the same second silently drop one of them. **Share links store NOTHING:** a link is the same `{target,name,filter}` base64url'd, so no token column, no revocation story, and the link survives the sender deleting their copy — stated in the migration rather than hidden: **a share link is a bearer of a QUERY, never of DATA** (whoever opens it still reads through Q66/Q64's policies). **One validator, both doors** — a JSONB row and a stranger's URL are equally untrusted, so `parseSavedViewPayload` serves the row read, the request body and the decoded link; a second copy is how "the shared link shows different rows than the saved view" gets written. **Two limits made one:** parse.ts's 64 KiB ceiling is now a CHECK on the column (IMMUTABLE fn, 0015 precedent) — without it a row is *storable but unreadable*, a view that saves then 400s on open. **Two partial unique indexes, not one (0017's NULL lesson again):** a single composite would reject duplicate TEAM names while allowing unlimited duplicate PERSONAL ones. **UTF-8 before base64** — `btoa` alone throws above U+00FF, so "Clientes — señales" would fail to share while ASCII worked; decode refuses standard base64 rather than translating it, and `fatal: true` refuses invalid UTF-8 instead of seeding U+FFFD into a name. **RLS on, no policies** — anon key is in the client bundle, so an RLS-less table is anon-*writable* on an already-open dashboard. **Prod-verified, not claimed:** tracked apply, `relrowsecurity = true`, live probe — good insert ACCEPTED, `unlit PEOPLE` vs `  Unlit people  ` rejected 23505, second distinct personal view ACCEPTED, valid team view ACCEPTED, and team-without-team / personal-with-team / unknown target / array filter / >64KiB filter / blank name each rejected 23514; the allow-list is exact, not `.endsWith(".twilio.com")` (which `api.twilio.com.evil.example` satisfies — pinned with three more lookalikes). **UNPLAYABLE IS NOT ABSENT** — no recording renders no player, an unplayable recording renders a sentence; Q68's read surfaces stay behind the Q64 gate.** **STILL UNTICKED: DoD is a real call on the timeline with a summary — all three keys are Rob's (PING-INBOX), no call has run.** 📐 ATLAS 2026-07-26: a new leaf with no edges yet — `callTimeline` detail (`recordingSid`+`recordingUrl`) → `recordingAudio` → *(the `/api/calls/recording` route and the `<audio>` element, both still unbuilt)*; built-not-wired. Atlas needs its refresh pass. **7/26 inc.29 (driver): WHAT OUR SERVER SENDS UPSTREAM, AND WHAT COMES BACK — the media-proxy seam (`lib/calls/recordingProxy.ts`, 13 tests, PRD 3.1.202).** dev_chat re-queried first — newest rob **#47** < newest max **#49**, nothing posted; Q64 still BLOCKED-ON-ROB and Q67b's remaining tick is that same Q64 deploy call, so Q68 stayed top actionable. inc.28 pointed the player at `/api/calls/recording?sid=…` and stopped — **that route does not exist**; this is the seam it is made of, pure, because the half of a media proxy that fails invisibly is not the `fetch`, it is **which headers travel in each direction**. **THE HOST IS RE-CHECKED WHERE THE CREDENTIAL IS** — inc.28's check ships in the browser bundle and protects the UI; the stored URL is a value a *webhook payload* put in our database. **THE CREDENTIAL IS BUILT ONLY AFTER THE HOST IS APPROVED, IN THE SAME FUNCTION** — so no code path in the system can produce an `Authorization` header for a non-allow-listed URL; a separate `authHeader()` helper is one careless call site away from being the leak (pinned: five hostile hosts incl. `api.twilio.com.evil.example` and the EC2 metadata IP, each refusal serialising without the secret). **RANGE TRAVELS BOTH WAYS AND A 206 STAYS A 206** — collapsed to 200, `<audio>` marks the stream unseekable: the jump list still renders, every row still says *4:12*, and clicking one moves the playhead **nowhere** (inc.28's bad-seek/no-seek rule one layer down, and the reason inc.23 chose segments over a blob). Range forwarded verbatim, never re-derived; a 206 with no `content-range` is refused rather than allowed to seek wrong. **HEADERS BY ALLOW-LIST, NEVER WHOLESALE** — a third party's `set-cookie` on our origin is a session-fixation primitive. **AN UPSTREAM 401 IS NOT OUR 401** — relaying it prompts the REP for a password to OUR dashboard over a failure between us and Twilio ("the dashboard logged me out"); 401/403/429/5xx → our 502, 404 stays 404. **AN HTML ERROR PAGE WITH A 200 IS NOT AUDIO** — piped through it is a player that loads successfully and is silent forever, which reads as a broken recording, not a broken fetch. **NO CREDENTIAL = 503, NOT 404** (inc.2's not-configured vs nothing-there). **NOTHING SHARED-CACHEABLE** — verbatim customer speech on an unauthenticated prod: a CDN copy outlives Q64's decision and is keyed by a URL anyone can construct, so `no-store` goes in both `cache-control` and `cdn-cache-control`, with `no-referrer`/`nosniff`/`inline`. 1614/1614 vitest (13 new), build green. **NO DEPLOY — no user-visible surface; Q68's read surfaces stay behind the Q64 gate.** **STILL UNTICKED: DoD is a real call on the timeline with a summary — all three keys are Rob's (PING-INBOX), no call has run.** 📐 ATLAS 2026-07-26: still a leaf, one hop longer — `recordingAudio` → `recordingProxy` → *(the `/api/calls/recording` route, unbuilt, and from it the first credentialed edge to api.twilio.com media)*; built-not-wired. Atlas needs its refresh pass.

- [ ] Q62. 🛒 **IN-CHAT COMMERCE AS AN MLE DELIVERABLE — SCOPE ONLY (ADD-ON; Rob dump #2, 2026-07-25 evening, `docs/plans/sources/ROB-CHAT-COMMERCE-DUMP-2026-07-25.md`, folded 2026-07-25 into DD tracker §7).** Rob: *"adding a payment processor to the MLE's deliverables, for example creating a website with a chatbot where the chatbot serves as a personal shopper and then the user can checkout right from within the chatbox."* **This is the PRODUCT-layer twin of Q60/Q61's BUSINESS-layer question — do not merge them.** Q60/Q61 decide whose paper Rob signs and what he earns; Q62 is what a client receives. **PLACEMENT IS DELIBERATE: below Q60/Q61 and below ALL P1 work** — §3g's rule binds here too (the payments layer is downstream of P1; an hour moved off P1 lowers the payments outcome). **PRIOR ART, NOT A COLD START (and NOT overwritten):** the 7/23 swarm already ranked chatbot-checkout rails (Square API payment links built for SMS/chat · Helcim in-widget HelcimPay.js · x402 for agentic USDC) and logged the 2025 agentic-commerce standards (Mastercard Agent Pay, Visa Intelligent Commerce/TAP, Google AP2); §3f mechanic #1 is *AIVA card-in-chat*. All of that was filed under **AIVA the product** — **never** under **MLE the deliverable**, which is the gap this item records. **✅ ~~BLOCKED-ON-ROB (which vertical?)~~ — CORRECTED BY ROB THE SAME EVENING, DD §7.5: there is NO vertical gate and Max's first read was wrong.** Rob: *"we have customers across every vertical… some of those will inevitably be ecommerce… I was just using an eCommerce site as an example."* **This is a HORIZONTAL capability on an add-on MLE already plans to ship** — the embedded chatbot rides the websites MLE spins up, AIVA gets trained per vertical (e-commerce being one training target), and the money moment varies while the capability does not: product purchase (retail) · deposit on a booked job (contractors) · fee payment (title/RE) · invoice/retainer (services). **The design principle is Amazon's Buy Now button next to Add to Cart — fewer steps, collect payment before the customer ever reaches a checkout page.** Rob's three reasons, verbatim: *"a convenience to the customer, a way to convert on more sales for the business owner, and a profit center for MLE."* | **DoD (scope, not code):** (1) a one-page spec of the in-chat flow written **once, horizontally**, with the money moment as a parameter (discover-or-quote → select-or-scope → in-chat pay → receipt → CRM activity), covering at minimum the four money moments above; (2) the rail named from the 7/23 shortlist (Square payment links / Helcim HelcimPay.js) with the **hard constraint written into the spec: card data never enters the LLM context or our logs** — hosted field / hosted page / tokenized element only, bot sees a token and a status, target posture **PCI SAQ-A** (DD §7.4, §7.7); (3) an answer to DD §5's new attach-rate line — a **sourced** attach figure for payments *delivered as product* vs. Apideck's banked *"often remain below 20%"* for payments *sold separately* (today it is a named hypothesis with **no number**, and no number gets invented); (4) placement decided — Phase-2+ add-on vs. a component in the Q40 Blueprint phase model — and written into the PRD. **HOUSE LIMITS:** nothing enters `docs/training/phase-one-explainer.md` §2 or any client-facing scope (that section is generated from signed agreements via `phase1_engine.py build_scope()`); no processor committed, no money field moved, nothing signed. **Rob's own WHY, folded 2026-07-25 pm (DD §7.7) — it answers the rung debate rather than opening a new one:** *"if I'm going to offer that service, I would like to get a piece of the profit from the processing fee… merchant accounts in your portfolio also have a high multiple attached for resale."* **He does not need to be an ISO or a PayFac to get that piece — the residual comes from the Schedule A, not the rung, so rung 1 pays it at $0.** What PFaaS buys (and neither the agent nor the ISO rung does) is MLE **owning** the in-chat checkout and programming the charge. Two knock-ons now tracked in DD §5: **NN#3 (third-party portfolio sale rights) is promoted to the deal-breaker clause** — Rob is buying a saleable asset, and §3d already caught SignaPay publishing *"portfolio buyouts"* meaning **itself as buyer**, which does not carry the 25–40× outside multiple — and **PFaaS revenue-share economics are UNSOURCED**, the one gap that keeps the ISO-vs-PayFac comparison from being honest before 8/8. **Design point Max is raising unprompted (not from Rob, flagged as Max's): for a real e-commerce client the in-chat Buy Now cannot be JUST a card charge.** An order taken in the chat has to land back in the merchant's store — inventory decrement, fulfillment, tax/shipping calc, order status, refunds — or MLE has created an off-books sale the client's Shopify/Woo/BigCommerce back office never sees. So the spec's storefront path needs a **store-integration leg** (order created via the platform's API, or the chat driving that platform's own checkout/cart-permalink) on top of the payment leg; the deposit/fee/invoice money moments do NOT carry this and are the simpler first build. This is a scoping fact, not an objection. **Note for whoever picks this up:** DD §7.3 is the reason it is worth doing at all — shipping checkout *as part of the deliverable* makes merchant boarding an onboarding step instead of a second close, which is the highest-leverage term in the §3g revenue model. §3f still binds: at the $0-agent rung this earns the residual but adds **zero** switching cost. **7/25 inc.9 (driver): THE SPEC EXISTS — `docs/plans/IN-CHAT-COMMERCE-SPEC.md`, DoD legs (1) and (2) CLOSED (PRD 3.1.133).** Picked as the top ACTIONABLE item — gate map re-verified unchanged (Q57 still on Rob's two one-word Title Base answers, newest rob dev-chat #47 < max #49; Q60 fully at its Rob/browser/outage/7-28-chase boundary; Q61's packet shipped and waits only on Rob's two calls; Q15/Q5b creds-blocked, Q16/Q6 Phase-4-gated, Q39–Q45 Rob-gated). **Leg (1):** five stages — discover-or-quote → select-or-scope → **pay-in-chat** → receipt → CRM activity — with stages 3+4 **identical in every vertical** (that is where the payments layer lives) and only 1, 2, 5b varying; all four money moments carried, and **build order stated against the pitch order** (deposit/fee/invoice first — one amount, no inventory; the storefront moment carries the §5 leg). Five invariants as rules not preferences: the bot **never invents an amount** (LLM-computed price out of scope by rule); idempotency issued at stage 2; every completed payment writes a CRM activity (no off-books money moment); decline/abandon is a designed branch and **status comes from the processor, never the conversation**; the customer can always leave and pay the normal way, so a broken chat can never block a sale. **Leg (2):** Square hosted payment links + Helcim HelcimPay.js from the 7/23 short-list, with the posture that **rail selection FOLLOWS the Schedule A** — a rail Rob can't board under his own code pays him nothing (§7.7); 2025 agentic standards tracked, not depended on. **The 🔴 non-negotiable is on the face of the spec:** card data never enters the LLM context, prompts, transcripts, logs or Supabase — hosted only, bot sees a token and a status; PCI target **SAQ-A**, a card field in our DOM is a **new decision** not an implementation detail, *"read me your card number"* prohibited **including as a fallback**, **non-collection is the control and redaction only a backstop**, and the same rule binds voice. **§5 carries Max's storefront finding** with the catch printed: shape (A) chat drives the platform's own checkout may produce the convenience with **NO residual**; shape (B) chat charges then writes the order via platform API must answer **partial failure (money captured, order write failed)** before any code. **Q62 stays UNTICKED and says why: leg (3) attach rate is still a hypothesis with NO number** (none invented) **and leg (4) placement is Rob's call.** House limits honoured: nothing signed, no processor committed, no money field moved, nothing entered `phase-one-explainer.md` §2 or any client-facing scope. Both sit inside the ***"20% to 90+%"*** vertical-SaaS spread published by a **named operator, Ronnie Gurion COO of Clio**, who also supplies the mechanism: payments as a **core product** in sales quotas → *"maximum attach"*, a **SPIFF** → it *"still gets treated like a secondary product"* — which is precisely the difference between MLE shipping a site that transacts and MLE referring a merchant. Stripe's 200+-company 2025 benchmark corroborates direction (median attach *"has doubled in 1 year"*; 87% vs 30%) and is weighted **medium** because the absolute median is unpublished and was not guessed. **Toast is recorded as a CONTRAST, not a benchmark** — ~100% attach by *contractual requirement*, third-party sourced. **The bound is printed on the face of both documents, not buried: there is NO published figure for our actual mode** (third-party-built site, chatbot takes the payment, merchant boarded on the *builder's* Schedule A) — Shopify is a first-party platform monetizing its own checkout, so **67% is a directional ceiling, never a forecast, and §3g's sub-20% denominator (19 orgs → ~4 merchants ≈ $140–200/mo) STANDS as the planning number.** **One number was deliberately NOT manufactured:** blending 67% with 20% into a "realistic" attach would be a fabricated statistic on a money model — the refusal is written into §3i so no later run can quietly invent it. **What it changes:** Q61 rec (4) gains a sourced range plus its honest bound, and chat-commerce is reclassified from *nice add-on* to **the single identified lever on the term that makes the whole payments add-on small** (~3× on §3g at constant customer count) — a **product** decision (Q62), **not** a rung decision (Q61); rec (1) untouched, still no argument for jumping a rung. §7.3's original hypothesis paragraph left as written with a dated UPDATE beneath it, not retro-edited. Second half of the same message is the **pre-sale Estimator**: *"have a field where we can input any amount of investment called Est Investment, then an input field where you can change the number of days so far in Phase 2 which will change all the numbers, list the top recommended automations underneith… figure out what type of employee would likely normally handle that task, what their hourly rate is in the region the business is in… estimate how many hours they usually spend on the task the automation is doing 24/7, estimate what… performing the task automatically would have garnered them in additional revenue… for each one of the automations recommended. Then show a summary."* | **DoD:** (1) spec written, formula + worked example + open questions — `docs/plans/PHASE2-ROI-ENGINE-SPEC.md`; (2) the arithmetic as a **pure, tested module** (CR-3 — no clock, no network, no UI re-derivation); (3) a **sourced** regional labor-rate table, every rate carrying its URL, fallback level always stated; (4) the **Estimator UI** — Est Investment + editable days, one row per recommended automation showing role/rate/region/BLS link/hours/lift/value, then the summary with ROI % and ROI $, green surplus / red shortfall, "day N of 91" visible; (5) the **running (actuals) ROI** wired to real Phase-2 data on the company record — **gated on Q40's phase model landing** (there is no "Phase 2 start date" field until then); (6) open question A answered by Rob (revenue attribution, below). **7/25 inc.11 (driver): LEGS (1)(2)(3) CLOSED — the engine is built, tested, and specced (PRD 3.1.136).** Recovered from an orphaned run that was cut off mid-increment: the dump, `lib/roi/phase2.ts`, `lib/roi/laborRates.ts` and `lib/__tests__/phase2Roi.test.ts` were all on disk, uncommitted, and referenced a spec file that **did not exist** — the dangling reference is now a real document and the whole thing is folded into the queue and the PRD. **Leg (2):** `computePhase2Roi` + `estimatePhase2Roi`, pure per CR-3, `guaranteeDays` parameterised because Rob said *"We might change this formula later"* — so 91 is a default, not a hard-code. Three honesty rules are in the arithmetic, not the UI: **day 0 returns `roiPct: null`**, never `Infinity` and never a fake `0%` (the $ is still shown, because that one IS defined); past day 91 the target **stops growing** and the result says `beyondGuaranteeWindow` rather than inflating forever; negative revenue (refund/chargeback) is allowed on purpose while negative hours throw. The Estimator's summary runs the **same** engine on the totals — labor passed as (total hours × blended rate) so the summary reproduces the per-automation total exactly instead of re-deriving it from one rate. **Leg (3):** `laborRates.ts` = **BLS OEWS May 2025 median hourly wage**, 9 roles an MLE automation actually displaces (reception, CSR, admin, bookkeeping, dispatch, social, marketing, outbound sales, telemarketing), at Naples-metro → Florida → national, **every row carrying its `bls.gov` URL** (house rule 10).
